ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e4253	Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e4268	In particular, support for young farmers is of particular importance. We have already set a priority in the last agricultural reform with special top-up measures in the common agricultural policy. Austria benefits fully from this and we have also planned this for the next CAP strategy plan. A total of 14 million euros are available for young transferees, for a maximum of the first 40 hectares supported by a top-up. But it is of particular importance to us in Austria, especially in the area of the branch premium. If young farmers take over the farm, they will receive appropriate support with higher professional agricultural skills. We will continue to do so in the future, and this is also enshrined in the CAP Strategy Plan. These two measures have also contributed in recent decades to the fact that there is actually a very high proportion of young farmers in Austria. We are certainly one of the best countries with around 35 percent young farmers planning to take over the farms in Austria in the future.

At the same time, we have already increased the seasonal quotas for agricultural harvesters last year and are trying, above all, to recruit workers in states such as the Western Balkans and the like.

We have put a very great emphasis on the fact that the municipalities join the entire municipality; in other words, we have designed the funding guidelines in such a way that the municipalities get a top-up if they join the municipality as fully as possible in order to ensure a faster coverage of broadband. The third area, which is crucial to us, is the 5G expansion in Austria. At the last 5G auction, we designed a tender design, which also includes supply requirements. That is to say, the telecom operators who increase licenses are expanding in the urban area, which is, of course, a business model for them, but at the same time they are obliged to make two thirds of the expansion in under-supplyed regions. We have been seeing for a year now that expansion is a lot faster, because for every single expansion in the urban area, two transmission systems will simply be built up accordingly in sub-supplyed regions.

Cohesion policy is active in cities and regions, in the form of investment in employment, growth, quality of life, in infrastructure, as initially said, that the regions are perceived as home to citizens. Cohesion policy is thus the idea of hundreds of thousands of projects in the regions of Europe, and thousands of projects have actually been implemented in Austria since accession to the European Union; more than 1,500 projects have been carried out in Styria alone. In today's decision of this 15a agreement with the countries under item 1 of the agenda, the final aim is to define the rules of the game in Austria: What tasks do the Federal Ministries - as Mrs Köstinger's Federal Ministry is particularly demanding, but also those of the Minister of Labour and the Minister of Social Affairs - and what tasks are to be fulfilled in the countries in order to obtain these resources so that these resources are used in accordance with the requirements of the European Union, so that at the end we feel and experience a strengthening of the regions? ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e7565	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. I note that the speeches by the honourable Members have relatively little to do with the current subject of negotiations. However, I would like to take a brief look at this, because this is a very important vote for us today. There is a government template for an Article-15a agreement between the Federal Government and countries on national resolution and control of EU cohesion policy. The Federal Ministry of Agriculture, Regions and Tourism has the function of overall coordination, and I must point out that regional and structural policy in Austria is not an independent competence of the Federal Constitutional Act. In Austria, this task is carried out by several competent Federal Ministries and, above all, by the countries; therefore, we now also need the present Article 15a agreement. This agreement has been negotiated and jointly developed for over two years with all the partners concerned on the federal and country sides. I should like to express my special thanks to the representatives of the Austrian Federal Council, who have been very much involved. In this 15a agreement, which in particular includes administrative and control agreements, three points are particularly important: On the one hand, it is the responsibility of the bodies of the administrative, monitoring and control system to be set up in accordance with EU Regulation, on the other hand, it is the procedural provisions for the implementation of programmes and, on the other hand, the responsibilities and procedures in the case of financial corrections. These relate to three relevant funds: This is one of the European Regional Development Fund, the Just Transition Fund and the European Social Fund Plus. Since the beginning of the negotiations on the EU regulations for the new programming period, we have also worked very intensively on simplifying the administrative arrangements on the part of the Federal Ministry. You, ladies and gentlemen, have always been confronted with questions in this regard. We have tried to implement this very concretely. In particular, the rules should relieve project promoters and the funding agencies in order to ensure that the quality of the projects is at the forefront and that rapid implementation is made possible. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e9487	Let us now go to agenda item 5. There was no oral reporting. I welcome Mr Martin Kocher to this debate in the House. Mrs Carina Reiter, you have the floor first. Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e9520	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, Dear viewers! Family support is an important form of family support in Austria. It is a direct service to compensate for costs incurred by child dependants. In 1955 the family compensation law was passed for the first time, and the intention of the law still has the same validity as at the time. The balance between the financial additional burden on the diet, clothing, domestic housing and education of children is not only a requirement in the sense of social justice, but also a social necessity. The time passes, the family burden compensation law exists. Since 1955, of course, a lot has happened. The world is more digital, the world has become more efficient, and so, of course, the family burden compensation law and the associated settlement procedures have developed further. Since March 2021, Fabian has been active in the family aid process. This reduces and reduces the effort on the one hand for citizens, but on the other hand also for employees in the financial office, so that the resolution becomes more efficient. The current item on the agenda we are discussing concerns the digital development of this family aid procedure. Through the processing of external data, the process is to become more efficient and faster. Thus, the resolution is facilitated. The processing of data for students, apprentices and students is implemented. The main key points are that the further payment of family aid for four months after completion of schooling – which means simply a continuous reference to family aid – is possible. Another important point is the processing of this data. We hope that today we will decide on the package two in which it concerns the data of the apprentices and the students in order to make this facilitation accessible. So what are the benefits for citizens? – This is an efficient assessment of claims. It is about a rapid automation-aided extension of family aid. No submission of paper documents or additional upload on financial online is necessary. No official routes are necessary in this form. It is simply a matter of quick and unbureaucratic handling of this procedure. In short, it is just about making families a little easier at a time that is often very stressful, which is very demanding – that is, school, profession, the challenges that everyday life brings with it – to simplify life, to the extent that it is technically possible, and thus simply to reduce this red tape for the families. So our credo is quite clear: so little as possible and only as much as necessary.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e11282	Madam President. Estimated Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear spectators at home and at the gallery! Four fundamental freedoms are the basis of our EU internal market: Services, capital, persons and goods may move freely through the Union territory. For us consumers, this is a great luxury, but we also need to look at the background. Because goods do not move by themselves, they must be transported by someone. This is still very common in the EU by means of trucks, and these are not yet on their own. It needs a person sitting in front of the cab. For these employees, the drivers, we have already decided over the past decades on important regulations to protect against exploitation. There are now new rules for cabotage within Europe. Foreign carriers shall no longer be allowed to send their drivers eternally and for three days through different Member States. At least once a month, drivers must spend their rest period in their home country. We are now also reorientating weekly rest periods. These are now to be planned and are no longer only to be spent in the truck. These are important steps for workers' protection and also against wage dumping, as it cannot be that domestic carriers are not competitive because foreign competitors are at the expense of their drivers. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] We are therefore taking further important steps to improve the working environment and the conditions of competition in the logistics industry. The scheme also includes adapting to the new driving licence conditions. L17 drivers now have the opportunity to get a driver's card and are thus also protected by the complete technical record. All of this is legal framework that we must create as a National Council. It is not the trade unions that are responsible and responsible for this, but we as politicians. ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e11698	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Dear ladies and gentlemen at home in front of the screens! There are two legal projects that are on the agenda today: on the one hand, the planned and now also economic exit from the corona-short work – the corona-short work, not the short work overall, I say a little more – and the mobility package for road safety related to regulations regarding rest periods and the like. Before I start, maybe a few words about the situation: We are experiencing a very difficult situation with great uncertainty, a Russian war of attack on Ukraine, which, I believe, makes us all very affected, also the consequences on the people there. Firstly, of course, the protection of these people in Ukraine and when they come to us, but of course the whole also has an impact on the labour market, even if we do not see a very strong effect at the moment as far as unemployment is concerned. We have experienced further declining unemployment rates over the last few weeks. The number is now as low as it was last year in summer, and in summer it is generally lower than in winter and spring, i.e. the situation is further stable and the labour market is resilient. However, the dynamics seems to be slowing down, and whatever happens, it may well be that the situation worsens further, that we will see in the labour market the consequences of sanctions, the consequences of the war and the consequences of the economic effects of this war, and that means, of course, that we have to prepare for it. An important factor will continue to be short work. That is why, I believe, it is important to develop this short work. There are three things that play a role today in connection with the short work. First of all, the corona short work: This was the short-time work for companies which were closed or where there were massive losses in sales due to the administrative restrictions. This corona short work has secured 1.3 million jobs in 120,000 establishments. Especially at the height of the pandemic, at the beginning, it was particularly important. It can now expire at the end of March, because the pandemic is not affecting the labour market so much. That's the first point. I think it is important to say that although the pandemic is still showing consequences, fortunately it is no longer so strong in the labour market. Secondly, There is regular short work running until the end of June. It is agreed to work together with the social partners on the way forward in the coalition on how this short work will take place from 1. ==References====External links== It is clear that there is still a need for short work. This is not only about supply bottlenecks, but also about sanctions that cause, for example, companies to be massively affected in their business. That is why this short-term work is needed to maintain employment. The third point concerns the duration of short work. There is a legal basis for short-term work to be limited to 24 months in principle for a short-term work project. There are some farms that were actually very, very long affected by corona restrictions. These are especially organizers, especially city hotels that could no longer organize events or congresses. For these, we now want to extend the maximum use by two months until 26 months in this legislative project, in order to make possible a planned and ordered exit from the corona-short work. I believe that this gives economic meaning and is also the optimal solution for all those concerned, for the companies and for the employees concerned. We are talking about a small number of five-digits, of just over 10,000 employees, who have so far been in short work almost over the entire 24 months. The second important point, which is not on the agenda but which is also related to business security, refers to a regulation which I would like to mention very briefly today. Today, together with the health sector, we have extended the Risk Block Exemption Regulation by two months until the end of May. The number of infections at Omikron is still high. The risk groups are often even more at risk of vaccination than the other workers. That is why, I think, it is the right signal to extend this exemption. It will only enter into force if there are no other possibilities such as home office or other protective measures, protected But a few thousand employees before very dangerous processes, which could result from an infection – therefore this extension by two more months. The costs then take over the public hand, i.e. the establishments have no cost when such exemption comes from risk group members. The last point, very briefly, concerns the mobility package. This is about improved protection standards for handlebars. It has already been mentioned: The weekly rest period should not be spent in the vehicle. There is therefore a ban, there are better accounting rules, modernisation of the record rules, and there is an even stronger obligation at regular rest periods. This will increase road safety, which is why I would also like to ask for approval of this sensible regulation. Thank you.

Now to the item on the agenda 11, to which we have not heard much from the previous speakers. It is about social security relations with the Canadian province of Quebec. Austria has agreements on social security with many different countries around the world. These include, in particular, provisions relating to pension insurance, equality between nationals of both sides or the aggregation of periods of insurance and residence for entitlement to benefits. In Canada, however, there is a speciality, which concerns the province of Quebec. This one has its own pension system. In addition, the health and accident insurance sectors in Canada fall within the competence of the provinces. That is why Austria has concluded a international agreement with Quebec in order to ensure social security there too. Now, of course, it is also true that various developments in national and intergovernmental social security areas require adjustments and revisions from time to time. That's how it's been with Canada. In 2015, a new agreement could be agreed. The present law now also updates the agreement with the province of Quebec. It concerns mainly new pension calculations and, in addition, the new provisions in the field of accident insurance. I'm asking for approval. Thank you.

Against this background, it is particularly shocking to look at the number of apprentices. While in 2008, 132,000 young people were in one teaching, it was only 107,000 in 2021, a 20 - percent minus. At the same time, open apprenticeships have exploded during this period. In 2008, “only” 3 600 teaching places were open, while 2021 more than 7 200 teaching places were open, the numbers doubled. So we have to do more and we have to put less on flakes, such as teaching more attractive – I almost can't hear it when it comes. We also have to fill these flakes with life when we use them! That is why I would like to make three very specific proposals for what we could do. First: We make teaching more attractive by making it more flexible. We no longer live in 1950 when we learned a profession and then practiced it all his life. We need to simplify the fact that one can switch from one teaching profession to another, so we need to modulate the teaching. Secondly, We need more, we need better, we need mandatory professional orientation. I'm saying this whole salopp this way: If you are in school at 14 or 15 years of age, it is not more sensible, for example two Work hours a week with a regular professional orientation – of course, this professional orientation has to be high-quality – to sit in sign or religious education for two hours? Let me give you a very specific example: I was in Upper Austria with a small and medium-sized company and spoke to the apprentices there. There was a young woman – I have to look at it again, because it is such a complex teaching profession – she has made a teaching as a heat, cooling and sound protection engineer, so something very technical. At the vocational orientation, she found out that she was supposed to be a bodywasher. So now nothing against bodywashers, but, I think that this is a bit practical and separate, everybody knows it. Thirdly, the FPÖ will now be less favoured, we will be able to close the apprenticeship gap not only with Austrian apprentices. That is why we have also made a very concrete proposal, namely to reform the Red and White Red Card and to introduce a separate residence title with the Red and White Red Card teaching so that young people from third countries can have the legal opportunity – that is not possible now – to come to Austria to learn a teaching profession here. I say it quite clearly: Yes, we need a welcome culture for qualified immigration. The teaching is the way out of the shortage of skilled workers. Our proposals are on the table, and now you have to finally get on with it!

No. 560/1978, as last amended by the Federal Law BGBl. I No. 32/2022, is amended as follows: 1. The following Sections 380b and 380c and headings are inserted after § 380a: ‘SARS-COV-2 antigen tests for own use § 380b. (1) The public pharmacies are entitled to issue SARS-COV-2-antigen tests for the purposes of self-use to eligible persons on behalf of the social insurance institution for the duration of the COVID-19-pandemic declared by the WHO. (2) The right of reference shall be: 1. all persons insured under this Federal Law, 2. persons according to § 5 para. 1 Z 1, 3. and the entitled relatives of the persons referred to in Z 1 and 2. A five-piece pack may be delivered to each eligible person per month. (3) The Sozialversicherungsanstalt has to pay a flat-rate fee of 10 euros per package delivered. Payments of persons entitled to benefits shall not be allowed. The Federal Government has to replace the resulting expenses from the COVID-19 crisis management fund. (4) The Social Insurance Institute is active in the transferred field of activity, with the instructions of the Federal Minister for Social Affairs, Health, Care and Consumer Protection. Honorary fee for the delivery of COVID-19 ropes § 380c. The Sozialversicherungsanstalt has to pay a flat-rate fee of 15 euros to the public pharmacies for the delivery of a cure provided and financed by the Federal Government for the treatment of COVID-19. By way of derogation from § 30b para. 1 Z 4 third last sentence ASVG does not require the prescription of such a remedy to be granted medically by the Chief and Control Officer.’ 2. The following section 398 and heading are added after section 397: ‘Exclusions relating to Art. 1 of the Federal Law BGBl. I No. xx/2022 § 398. (1) The public pharmacies are entitled to issue SARS-COV-2-antigen tests for the purposes of self-use to eligible persons on behalf of the insurance institution for the duration of the COVID-19-pandemic declared by the WHO. I No. xx/2022 enters retroactively with 21. ==References====External links== September 2022 except for force.’; Reasons In the same tone as the amendment in 2. Reading on 1781/A, its content (enlargement of the circle of persons entitled for the SARS-COV-2 antigen tests for personal use, the authorisation of regulations for the Federal Minister for Social Affairs, Health, Care and Consumer Protection to extend the determination by the end of 2022 as well as fees for the delivery of COVID-19 liftings in public pharmacies and medical home pharmacies) is also to be included in the Special Laws GSVG, BSVG and B-KUV. To Art. 1 to 3, each Z 1 and 2 (§ 380b GSVG, 374b BSVG and 261b B-KUVG): The circle of persons who are allowed to obtain SARS-COV-2 antigen tests for their own use in accordance with §§ 380b GSVG, 374b BSVG and 261b B-KUV should be extended to ensure that the limitation to certain birth years is avoided. In addition, in the future, those persons who are entitled to work on the basis of a corresponding application of the legal professional representation pursuant to § 5 para. 1 Z 1 GSVG are excluded from statutory health insurance unless they are insured under the provisions of a federal law. The measure is provisionally limited to the end of June 2022. ==References====External links== In June 2022, the Federal Minister for Social Affairs, Health, Care and Consumer Protection has the power to do so by means of a regulation. the withdrawal until 31 December of this year. ==References====External links== To Art. 1 to 3, each Z 1 (§§380c GSVG, 374c BSVG and 261c B-KUVG): According to medical expertise, the use of cures for the treatment of COVID-19 is also possible and appropriate in the established area. The medicinal products should be used in patients who have been tested positively for COVID-19 and who have an increased risk of severe disease. These are health care, which is part of the health insurance claim against the health insurance institution. The delivery of medicinal products is provided for in the public pharmacies in the established area. Due to the central procurement and financing of the cures by the federal government, the benefits of the public pharmacies can be paid. the wholesale trade does not rely on the usual pricing mechanisms (e.g. pharmaceutical tax, refund code arrangements), which is why the need to create its own legal basis for this particular case. Furthermore, in the present case, no approval by the health insurance institution's chief and supervisory service should be provided. This flat-rate fee is EUR 15 for each cure provided and includes the cost of distribution through the wholesale as well as the entire logistical effort up to advice and delivery. In view of the fact that, as outlined above, the delivery of the cures is health care in the sense of social security, the fees must be borne by the Austrian Health Fund. A prescription fee is not to be cancelled as it is in accordance with § 136 para. 4 ASVG is the treatment of an admissible communicable disease. [[Quoted print version removed]]

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e16263	Mr President. Minister! High house! Dear Seers! The Wiener Reparierungbonus is a success story, it pursues environmental, social and consumer protection goals. In the three action periods starting in September 2020, the Viennans and Viennaers were able to apply for a bonus online. Once they got it, they were able to redeem it within a certain time frame in the repair network – that is, 90 plants with over 100 locations. Around 50 percent of the gross invoice amount was funded up to a maximum of 100 euros. This unbureaucratic support enabled 32 000 devices to be repaired. This means saving incredible 770 tons of CO2. What does that mean in fact? – If all washing machines, notebooks, tablets and smartphones were to be extended by one year in the EU, this would mean a savings of 4 million tonnes of CO2 – as many as 2 million cars would save on our roads if they didn't drive a day. The Vienna model of the repair bonus also convinced the US-large city of Portland, which it introduced. The state of Washington is also considering implementing it. Not only Vienna promotes repairs, there are also other federal states that implement this. We as SPÖ therefore support the request to extend the Vienna repair bonus to all of Austria. Our motto is not to have a spotter in Austria, but to implement it uniformly. What is sad about this government: If something works well in Vienna, it does not yet mean that it works all over Austria. We have seen it in the belt tests, which have really worked super in Vienna, in other federal states we have had to wait for our results for days. Ladies and gentlemen, what is against extending the Vienna repair bonus to all of Austria? It works in Vienna.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e16669	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, Mr. Federal Minister! Estimated listeners at home in front of the screens! In any case, the repair bonus is a success story that can't be bad. The main objective of the repair bonus is, of course, to extend the lifetime of products, to maintain the value added of repair services in the region and, as mentioned above, to protect or support businesses that also train apprentices and offer repairs, and ultimately to reduce the amount of waste. Why is it so important to look exactly at this area, the field of electrical and electronic equipment? – Because the amounts of waste in this area are, among other things, those – in addition to textiles – that rise strongly. The repair bonus is a part that contributes to this or which has the potential to reduce this waste, so to speak, significantly. Let's look at the quantities: In the case of electrical and electronic equipment, the amount of waste actually increases very strongly, and this includes three main problems: On the one hand, it is a complex mixture of materials, with dangerous ingredients that I would like to address and which are also an environmental pollution, and the components and ingredients are also an environmental pollution from production. Therefore, it makes a lot of sense to look at exactly this segment of products. The separate collection is currently being built through the lock-up collection, via the problem collection in the municipalities; Electronic devices can be returned there, but also in specialized trade, because it is important that electrical appliances can also be returned there. It is precisely because everything you can fix before that, as it were, reduces the great effort of this waste fraction. [[Congratulations on Members of the Greens and the ÖVP.]] There are currently 40 plants in operation for the first treatment of these wastes, and there is evidence of why it makes so much sense to extend the life span. Since the beginning of this collection, some 1.1 million tonnes of old equipment have been collected in Austria, and it makes sense to look more closely at what the ingredients are. Nearly 600 000 tons of metals, i.e. non-ferrous metals such as copper and aluminium and various other metals, could be recycled – that is, it makes sense to extract and reuse exactly these fractions – but also 400 000 tons of plastics and glass and at least 3.2 tons of gold. Of course, it is important to preserve all these valuables, not to allow them to become waste prematurely and to keep the equipment containing all these substances in use for as long as possible. The repair bonus is an important contribution, a building block towards the circular economy, for longer service life. But above all, these are the sustainability criteria in public procurement, because the Sustainable Procurement Action Plan contains exactly this point: that in the procurement process – and that is now mandatory for all federal authorities since the last summer – specifications for IT and electrical appliances are required, among other things, a availability of spare parts – which is an important part – for at least four to seven years, but also the recycling capacity of plastics and other materials. The step in the direction of prolonged life, reducing the amount of waste, reducing the pollutants and forcing the circular economy – that is exactly where the repair bonus is in the right place.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e18382	Mr President! Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! It has already been said: We are discussing the first activity report on the Investment Control Act, which we agreed here in 2020 and which has been in force since then. The aim of this Investment Control Act is to review direct investment from outside the EU in Austria. The background is that there are always conflicts in areas where, so to speak, security and public order are at risk, namely in the energy sector – which is an important issue at the moment – but also in defence goods and in digital infrastructure. These are very sensitive areas, so we must ensure that we have control of these companies in Austria or Europe. This is the background of this Investment Control Act. The authorisation requirement applies to large companies and small enterprises are excluded. It's, I think, important that you don't act too rigid. It is therefore always a consideration between sufficient control on the one hand, but also user-friendliness; the growth of businesses should not be hindered. There were 50 procedures that were examined. Interestingly, I believe that only two conditions have been issued. This is probably because companies, if they knew they had no chance or it would be difficult, then my knowledge often withdrawn the applications. By the way, to the Sunset clause, which was mentioned: This applies precisely to one area, namely research in the field of medicinal products, vaccines and personal protective equipment, in other words in an important area. There is – under quotations – the “risk” that this range falls from the 10-percent threshold to the 25 percent control threshold. I welcome the fact that we can evaluate this and then decide together whether we want this Sunset clause to run out. On the topic of Russia, I would like to consider: Yes, we do not review every investment in the country, and especially Russia seems to have invested largely in money, but hardly created jobs, as this report clearly demonstrates. Russia is, therefore, the second largest investor in Austria, according to Germany, but there are only about 800 jobs dependent on these Russian investments. So, for example, real estates are purchased, and it is, I say, created little added value for Austria. Now, if we look the other way around, how much Austria is investing in Russia: We invest a fifth of what Russia is investing in Austria in Russia. 21 billion euros is invested by Russia in Austria, we are only 4.6 billion euros in Russia, depending on these investments, but 50 000 jobs – that is, a completely different relationship, a completely different added value. We can discuss whether we want to continue to handle this. Not yet mentioned was the case of Borealis, also a very up-to-date case: Eurochem, i.e. a company owned or controlled by a Russian oligarch – Melnichenko – wanted to buy the fertiliser segment of Borealis – i.e. plant nutrients, melamine and technical nitrogen. This would have been very critical, because it is about fertilizers. Borealis then decided in the final effect to stop this sale. That was exactly the period when the war in Ukraine had already begun. Two weeks later, this sale was stopped, so to speak. In other words, the Investment Control Act did not break down at all. In this sense: I think it's a very good, very sensible law. It controls where it is critical, and prevents purchases that we do not want to have, because we are losing influence and thus control in critical infrastructure areas where it is important for Austria. Thank you.

In Germany, for example, there is no such thing as that. So, unlike in the EIA procedure, we have set a clear maximum time limit. Even if there is an in-depth examination, a maximum of two months is possible. If there's no answer, then it's approved. So we have a mechanism that does not lead to delays. In addition, every entrepreneur knows that he should contact the authority as early as possible in a so-called due diligence – that is the sale phase. This is so in Germany, in the USA, in Japan, in Canada. That is to say, both lawyers and those responsible for the sale of the companies themselves know this very well. What we can do, however, is to move households to support us in energy efficiency. Experts say if you reduce only 1 degree in heating, you would save 6 percent in the heating bill. This is a way I am saying that this is a good measure of efficiency that we can support. We can implement all these topics strategically in the long term. But what do we need in the short term? – In the short term, we need security of supply for Austria. This is a measure that we are creating a strategic gas reserve, and my colleague Lukas Hammerno will make an amendment. To this end, the Federal Government is taking the measure that we want to create a stockpile to ensure sufficient energy for our businesses and for our households. Therefore, the AGGM is commissioned to deposit strategic gas reserves. This deposit is based on the January consumption of 2022. We would like to store 12.6 terawatt hours for the household and for our businesses. We want to ensure that, if sudden disturbances arise, we can ensure gas supply. The Federal Government is thus making an investment of approximately 1.6 to 2 billion euros available – that is what the price of gas is to be bought at. Other measures that we have already taken are of course also – we want to hold this –: We have launched an anti-proliferation package, and we also discussed it yesterday to decide on another package of measures. These two action packages comprise EUR 3.7 billion. In addition to the gas reserve, which will also be an investment of the federal government, this is more than EUR 5 billion, which the Federal Government is investing to ensure security of supply and durability. I would like your consent here. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e19189	Mr President. Dear Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen! We're in an extremely serious situation, and it's not a normal time either. None of us, I think, have any desire to transfer these criminals even one cent, but the bitter truth is that we are dependent on these Russian gas imports. At least we cannot give up on them in the short term. I feel this dependence – and how much this hurts, we all feel. I can only speak for myself and also for my group: We would like to feel this pain, this dependence never again. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We don't want to be dependent on Russian gas imports anymore. We don't want to have to buy Russian gas anymore. So what? [[Intrusion of the Abg. Matznetter.]] Of course, we now have the responsibility to diversify our gas imports in the short term. We need to look at other sources. But we must also be so honest as to say that this will not bring us much further. It will reduce our extreme dependence on Russian imports, but if we look at which other countries these gas imports can come from, when we look at who has the largest gas reserves in the world – Iran, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, Turkmenistan – then we find that these are not exactly sustainable alternatives. In other words, we need not only to get out of Russian gas, but we need to get out of all the gas imports and also from all the fossil energy imports. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We need effective measures in the long term, such as a heat law, with which we decide to withdraw from oil and gas heating and legally anchor it. We know that every gas heating we exchange today is a step towards this independence. We need a national power act to do this. But one thing is also clear – and we talked about it during the recovery of this law: We won't do this from today to tomorrow. As much as we try, we will not be able to give up natural gas in the next winter. We therefore have a responsibility for our country, so that if something really happens, if Russia turns to the gas tap or if the pipelines are damaged or sabotaged, which can simply be the case in a war, we are prepared. We have discussed a lot in recent weeks that the Austrian gas storage units are so empty. That's right, we have a gas storage level of about 15 percent, which is empty. It was only very quickly pointed out to the Federal Government, although we all know that, contrary to the situation in the oil sector, politics currently has no legal instrument to store gas itself or to require anyone to have a gas storage or gas storage system. Unfortunately, this has been left to the market in the past, and at the high gas prices we have had since last autumn, it is no wonder that the gas storage stations have been very low. The market has not helped us in this crisis as it has often been. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] So we must do everything we can to ensure that people in our country do not have to be afraid that they will not be able to heat in the next winter because we are simply and simply out of the gas. That is why we are now adopting a national strategic gas reserve, and I am submitting the following request, namely the amendment tabled by Mr Lukas Hammer, Mr Tanja Graf, Mr Cot, on the report from the Committee on Economic, Industrial and Energy on Amendment No 2359/A by Mr Ing. Martin Litschauer, Tanja Graf, colleagues concerning a federal law amending the Gas Economic Act 2011 in 1392 of the Supplements. The request has been submitted and distributed, so I will only explain it in its basics. With this amendment of the Gas Economic Act, we decide to build a national strategic gas reserve. As distribution area manager of the Republic of Austria, Austrian Gas Grid Management AG is commissioned to purchase and store more than 12 terawatt hours of gas. This is the gas consumption in a winter month. The gas reserve is really only used in the extreme emergency, if there is an energy steering case, no gas flows anymore and we depend, as it were, on getting over the winter. This is not to be confused with an obligation on the energy supply companies, which are also obliged to provide sufficient gas for their customers. We have also involved the National Council in this national gas reserve – Mr Kassinger and Mr Schroll – in that, in the case of possible adjustments to the amount of the gas reserve, but also in the case of discussions on the release of the reserves of the main committee, and this should also be decided on by a majority of two-thirds. There is also a report to be prepared annually and forwarded to the National Council, which reports on the use and procurement of the strategic gas reserve. The provisions on the strategic gas reserve will be evaluated in two years and will in principle expire at the end of September 2025, but the main committee can decide on the further use of the gas reserve, so what will happen to it. We know that there are also intensive discussions at European level on European regulations. We are now going ahead, but we may have to adapt this to the European rules. You might look at it to me: This law is now no reason for me to have a great day of joy. I think it is a necessity because, as a policy, we have a responsibility for the security of our country's supply, but it is not a law that gives me great pleasure. Especially great will I be when I stand here again before we will decide on the Renewable Warm Law, since it will be for me such a middle finger for the Russian warriors with whom we can signal them: We will no longer need your gas, we will no longer buy your gas and we will no longer finance your army with our gas bills! - Thank you. This toll flight mainly affects the resident population whose quality of life is seriously affected by heavy traffic. Not only exhaust gases and noise have become partly unbearable for the people in the area, but the dramatic increase in traffic in the affected communities also poses the danger of traffic accidents, especially for kindergarten children, toddlers, schoolchildren, older people with restrictions on movement and handicaps. At present, the narrow and rigid conditions of § 43 of the STVO do not provide for any means of regulation to prevent this adverse circumvention traffic. There is simply a lack of a legal basis, which requires it for a quality control of road transport from the point of view of the municipalities, the local economy as well as the locals. Therefore, I consider the goal of the petition to allow a ban on toll refugees to be legal and to achieve a change in the StVO, from the point of view of the municipalities, to be absolutely correct. It can't be that the cost savings so many fishers on the back of the population are burdened with the safety of our children. ParlaMint-AT_2022-03-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00149_d8e22414	Madam President. Dear colleagues! Fathers. Today, I am speaking to the petition “lock with hiding the designation of origin. ” Nowadays, more and more people want to know where their food comes from, whether they have been produced environmentally friendlyly and without genetic engineering. Finally, you also want to know what you are doing to your body. Mandatory designation of origin is currently only required for certain foods, such as fresh fruit, fresh vegetables, eggs, packed beef, pigmeat, sheepmeat, goatmeat and poultry. In the case of fish and fishery products, the fishing area must be specified when fished in the sea. In the case of olives, honey and all organic products covered by the EU bio-label, a designation of origin is also required. Dear ladies and gentlemen! The minimum font size regulation on origin marking adopted at EU level 2011 is uniformly regulated across the EU, but It is often really tiny. Unfortunately, the general minimum font size regulation of 1.2 millimeters allows easy hiding. In the case of smaller packaging, where the packaging plate is less than 80 square centimeters, the label shall be only 0,9 millimeters. The whole thing is almost like a picture search puzzle, as a consumer you have to look meticulously where the product comes from and work on it. I cannot accept the representation of the European Commission in Austria, which, in its opinion on my petition, has guaranteed clarity, legibility and information for consumers, in any case, and therefore I strongly oppose hiding the designation of origin. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Many manufacturers strive to give the product an Austrian paint. The use of red and white red flags is intended to give the impression that it is a product from Austria, although it is not. The Austrian Consumer Information Association also opposes some intentional hiding by highlighting misleading references to Austria. The opinion of the Ages shows that 79 samples have been examined throughout Austria in relevant priority actions. However, in the course of the review, a sample found that the font size had not been observed. I would like to mention again clearly and clearly that it is not the problem to keep the font size, but that we are responsible for creating the appropriate framework conditions in order to provide consumers with the greatest safety in order to facilitate the purchase decision. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] I therefore appeal again to all and, above all, to the Federal Minister for Consumer Protection and to the Federal Minister for Agriculture that the font size for the designation of origin should be adapted accordingly. In her opinion, Ms Köstinger said that she wanted to work hard in her government programme to improve the designation of origin. Mrs. Köstinger and Mr. Federal Minister for Smoking must commit and strengthen themselves in the European Union to the relevant bodies. Thank you.
